, and we use the shorthand
H^{*,*}(-)\coloneqq H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{*,*}(-)
for the mod-
\ell
motivic cohomology.

We want to compute the mod-
\ell
Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
following the same lines as
[
22
, Chapters 4-5-6]
. We first need to briefly recall the construction of the mod-
\ell
motivic Steenrod algebra, introduced in
[
49
]
.

5.1.
The motivic Steenrod algebra

Let us recall that a bistable cohomology operation of bidegree
(i,j)
on a motivic spectrum
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
is a family
\{\phi_{p,q}\}_{p,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of natural transformations of functors in
\phi_{p,q}:{\mathbb{E}}^{p,q}(-)\to{\mathbb{E}}^{p+i,q+j}(-)
that commute with arbitrary suspensions. The set of all bistable cohomology operations on
{\mathbb{E}}
has a natural ring structure induced by composition, which makes it a bigraded
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra. By
[
18
, Theorem 1.1]
, the algebra of bistable cohomology operations on
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
is naturally isomorphic to the
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra of shifted endomorphisms of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
,
\text{End}_{{\operatorname{SH}}(k)}^{*,*}(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell):=[H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell,\Sigma^{*,*}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(k)}
, where
\Phi:H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\to\Sigma^{i,j}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
defines the cohomology operation
\phi:=\{\phi_{p,q}:=\Sigma^{p,q}\Phi_{*}\}_{p,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

The short exact sequence

0\to{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\xrightarrow{\cdot\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{2}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\to 0

induces the distinguished triangle

(5.1)

H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\to H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{2}\to H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\xrightarrow{\beta_{0,0}}\Sigma^{1,0}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell

in
{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
. The resulting map
\beta_{0,0}:H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\to\Sigma^{1,0}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
in
{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
is the
Bockstein homomorphism
. This defines a bistable cohomology operation
\beta=\{\beta_{p,q}:=\Sigma^{p,q}\beta_{0,0}\}_{p,q}
of bidegree
(1,0)
on
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
, called the
Bockstein operator
, which satisfies the following properties:

(1)

\beta\circ\beta=0,

(2)

\beta(xy)=\beta(x)\cup y+(-1)^{p}x\cup\beta(y)
, for
x\in(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)^{p,*}(-)

(see
[
49
, Section 8]
).

In
[
49
, Section 9]
, for
i\geq 0
, Voevodsky defines reduced power operations
P^{i}
, with
P^{0}=1
, which we can consider as bistable cohomology operations of bidegree
(2i(\ell-1),i(\ell-1))
on
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
.

Definition 5.1
.

The
motivic mod-
\ell
Steenrod algebra

A^{*,*}=A^{*,*}(k,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)
is the subalgebra of the algebra of bistable cohomology operations on the mod
\ell
motivic cohomology
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
over
k
generated by the Bockstein operator
\beta
, the reduced power operations
\{P^{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
and operations
x\mapsto a\cdot x
for
a\in(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)^{*,*}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)
.

In fact,
A^{*,*}
is isomorphic to the entire algebra
\text{End}_{{\operatorname{SH}}(k)}^{*,*}(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)
of bistable cohomology operations on
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
(see
[
18
, Therem 1.1 (1)]
). In characteristic
0
, this was proved by Voevodsky (
[
48
, Theorem 3.49]
).

The bigraded algebra
A^{*,*}
is a left
H^{*,*}
-module via
(a\cdot\phi)(x):=a\cdot(\phi(x))
for
a\in H^{*,*}
,
\phi\in A^{*,*}
and
x\in H{\mathbb{Z}}^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})
.

A^{*,*}
is equipped with a map
\Delta:A^{*,*}\to A^{*,*}\otimes_{H^{*,*}}A^{*,*}
defined by

\Delta(\gamma)=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{i}
, where the bistable operations
\alpha_{i}
,
\beta_{i}
are determined by the relation

\gamma(x\cup y)=\sum_{i}(-1)^{ab_{i}}\alpha_{i}(x)\cup\beta_{i}(y),

for
x\in H^{a,a^{\prime}}({\mathcal{E}})
and
\beta_{i}
of bidegree
(b_{i},b^{\prime}_{i})
. Voevodsky also defines an
H^{*,*}
-submodule
(A^{*,*}\otimes_{H^{*,*}}A^{*,*})_{r}
for which the formula
(x\otimes y)\cdot(x^{\prime}\otimes y^{\prime})=xx^{\prime}\otimes yy^{\prime}
gives a ring structure, and which contains the image of
\Delta
, and calls a
coproduct
, which an abuse of notation, the co-associative map

(5.2)

\Psi:A^{*,*}\to(A^{*,*}\otimes_{H^{*,*}}A^{*,*})_{r},

(see
[
49
, Lemmas 11.6-11.9]
for the details).

In
[
49
, Section 13]
, Voevodsky defines the
Milnor basis
for
A^{*,*}
, given by cohomology operations
\rho(E,R)
, for
E=(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\ldots)
a sequence of zeros and ones that are almost all zeros, and
R=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},\ldots)
a sequence of non-negative integers that are almost all zero. Let us write
Q(E)\coloneqq\rho(E,\underaccent{\bar}{0})
and
\mathcal{P}^{R}\coloneqq\rho(\underaccent{\bar}{0},R)
. For the sequence
e_{i}=(0,0,\ldots,0,1,0,\ldots)
having
1
only in the
i
-th place, he defines
Q_{i}\coloneqq Q(e_{i})
. In this basis, we have
P^{i}=\mathcal{P}^{i\cdot e_{1}}
and
Q_{0}=\beta
.

The motivic Steenrod algebra
A^{*,*}
is the algebraic analogue of the classical Steenrod algebra
A^{\text{top}}\coloneqq A^{{\operatorname{top}}}_{\ell}
described in
[
25
]
. Analogously tp
A^{*,*}
,
A^{\text{top}}
acts on the singular cohomology
H^{*}(-,{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)
, has a Bockstein operator
\beta_{{\operatorname{top}}}
(see
[
44
, Chapter IV]
), power operations
P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}
, and Milnor basis
\rho^{{\operatorname{top}}}(E,R)
. We let
Q^{{\operatorname{top}}}(E)\coloneqq\rho^{{\operatorname{top}}}(E,\underaccent{\bar}{0})
,
\mathcal{P}_{{\operatorname{top}}}^{R}\coloneqq\rho^{{\operatorname{top}}}(\underaccent{\bar}{0},R)
and
Q_{i}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\coloneqq Q^{{\operatorname{top}}}(e_{r})
. We have
P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}=\mathcal{P}^{i\cdot e_{1}}_{{\operatorname{top}}}
,
P_{{\operatorname{top}}}^{0}=1
, and
Q_{0}^{{\operatorname{top}}}=\beta_{{\operatorname{top}}}
.

We now recall a few properties of the power operations.

Lemma 5.2
.

(1)

P^{0}={\operatorname{id}}
, and, for any
i<0
,
P^{i}=0
.

(2)

For
x\in H^{2n,n}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)
,
P^{n}(x)=x^{\ell}
.

(3)

For
x\in H^{p,q}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)
,
n>p-q
and
n\geq q
, we have
P^{n}(x)=0
.

(4)

Q_{i}
has bidegree
(2\ell^{i}-1,\ell^{i}-1)
, and for each
k\geq 1
,
P^{k\cdot e_{i}}
has bidegree
(2k(\ell^{i}-1),k(\ell^{i}-1))
.

Proof.

(1)
is
[
49
, Theorems 9.4-9.5]
.
(2)
is
[
49
, Lemma 9.8]
.
(3)
is
[
49
, 9.9]
. The formulas for the bidegrees of
Q_{i}
and
P^{k\cdot e_{i}}
come directly from their definitions in
[
49
, Section 13]
.
∎

Finally, let us define
B\subset A^{*,*}
as the
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-subalgebra generated by
\{Q_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
, and
M_{B}\coloneqq A^{*,*}/A^{*,*}(Q_{0},Q_{1},\ldots)
as the quotient of
A^{*,*}
by its left ideal generated by the special elements
Q_{i}
. Analogously, we have
B^{{\operatorname{top}}}\subset A^{{\operatorname{top}}}
and
M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\coloneqq A^{{\operatorname{top}}}/A^{{\operatorname{top}}}(Q_{0}^{{\operatorname{top}}}Q_{1}^{{\operatorname{top}}},\ldots)
.

Remark 5.3
.

Taken
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
and
u\in H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n,n}({\mathbb{E}})
, the left
A^{*,*}
-module structure on
H{\mathbb{Z}}^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})
gives the bigraded map
r_{u}:A^{*,*}\cdot u\to H{\mathbb{Z}}^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})
of left
A^{*,*}
-modules, defined by
r_{u}(\alpha\cdot u):=\alpha(u)
. If
H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2*+1,*}({\mathbb{E}})=0
, then
r_{u}
descends to a map of
A^{*,*}
-modules
r_{u}:M_{B}\cdot u\to H{\mathbb{Z}}^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})
. Indeed, since
Q_{i}
has bidegree
(2\ell^{i}+1,\ell^{i})
,
Q_{i}(u)
lands in
H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2(\ell^{i}+n)+1,\ell^{i}+n}({\mathbb{E}})=0
for each
i
. An analogous statement holds for
M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
in the topological setting.

5.2.
The
\text{Ext}_{A^{**}}
-algebra of
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})

Let us recall that, for two bigraded
A^{*,*}
-modules
N^{**},M^{**}
, we have the trigraded abelian group
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{**}}(N^{**},M^{**})
defined by

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{**}}^{a,(b,c)}(N^{**},M^{**}):={\operatorname{Hom}}_{D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{**})}^{b,c}(N^{**},M^{**}[a]),

where
D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{**})
denotes the derived category of bigraded
A^{**}
-modules, and
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{**})}^{b,c}(-,-)
is the
(b,c)
bigraded component of
{\operatorname{Hom}}_{D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{**})}(-,-)
. This gives a natural tri-grading on the sum
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{**}}^{*,(*,*)}(N^{**},M^{**})=\oplus_{a,b,c\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{**}}^{a,(b,c)}(N^{**},M^{**})
(we will omit the tri-grading from the notation). In particular, if we have
{\mathbb{E}},{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
and two maps
H^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})\to H^{*,*}[a]
and
H^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}}^{\prime})\to H^{*,*}[b]
in
D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{*,*})
, we get a map

H^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}})\otimes_{H^{*,*}}H^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}}^{\prime})\to H^{*,*}\otimes_{H^{*,*}}H^{*,*}[a][b]=H^{*,*}[a+b]

in the derived category of
(A^{*,*}\otimes_{H^{*,}}A^{*,*})_{r}
-modules, and through the map
\Psi
introduced in (
5.2
), we can see this map in the derived category
D_{\text{bgr}}(A^{*,*})
.

Now, the description of the cohomology of
{\operatorname{MSp}}
given in Theorem
2.68
shows that
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is a free
H^{*,*}
-module, in particular flat, and by Corollary
4.10
the canonical map

H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})\otimes_{H^{*,*}}H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})\to H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}})

is an isomorphism. Therefore we have a product

(5.3)

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\otimes_{H^{*,*}}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\to{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*}).

Finally, the pullback
\mu^{*}_{\operatorname{MSp}}
of the multiplication map gives the map

(\mu_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{*})^{*}:{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\to{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*}),

and composing
(\mu_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{*})^{*}
with (
5.3
) gives the product

(5.4)

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\otimes_{H^{*,*}}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\to{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*}).

The co-associativity of the coproduct
\Psi
makes (
5.3
) associative, thus (
5.4
) is associative. We have then a natural structure of trigraded associative
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra on
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
. Following the strategy used in
[
22
]
for the case of MGL, we want to find a presentation for this trigraded
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra.

Definition 5.4
.

A
partition

\omega
of an integer
n\geq 0
is a sequence of integers
(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r})
with
\omega_{1}\geq\omega_{2}\geq\ldots\geq\omega_{r}>0
such that
|\omega|:=\sum_{i}\omega_{i}=n
. We say that a partition
\omega
is
even
if all the terms
\omega_{i}
are even.

For a positive integer
k
, let us extend the natural bigrading on
H^{**}
to a bigrading on the polynomial algebra
H^{*,*}[t_{1},\ldots t_{k}]
by giving
t_{i}
bidegree
(2,1)
. As before, we have the polynomial ring
H^{*,*}[b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}]
with
b_{i}
of bidegre
(4i,2i)
, and we get a map from
H^{*,*}[b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}]
to the subalgebra of symmetric polynomials in
t_{1}^{2},\ldots,t_{k}^{2}
by taking
b_{s}
to the elementary symmetric polynomial

f_{s}\coloneqq\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}}t_{i_{1}}^{2}\cdot\cdot\cdot t_{i_{s}}^{2}

for all
s\leq k
. Also, to each even partition
\omega=(2q_{1},\ldots,2q_{s})
of an even integer
2q
, we can associate the symmetric polynomial

f_{\omega}\coloneqq\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}}t_{i_{1}}^{2q_{1}}\cdot\cdot\cdot t_{i_{s}}^{2q_{s}}.

Since the
H^{*,*}
-algebra of symmetric polynomials in
t_{1}^{2},\ldots,t_{k}^{2}
is the polynomial algebra over
H^{*,*}
in the elementary symmetric polynomials in the same variables, we have a uniquely defined element
P_{\omega}\in H^{*,*}[b_{1},\ldots,b_{k}]
corresponding to
f_{\omega}
under the association
b_{s}\mapsto f_{s}
. For each even partition
\omega
, let
u_{\omega}
denote the element in
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
corresponding to
P_{\omega}
, using Theorem
2.68
to identify
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
with
H^{*,*}[b_{1},b_{2},\ldots]
. In
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
,
u_{\omega}
has bidegree
(2|\omega|,|\omega|)
.

Since
H^{a,b}=0
if
a>b
or if
b<0
by Theorem
2.8
, we have
H^{2*+1,*}=0
. As
b_{i}
has bidegree
(4i,2i)
, it follows that
H^{2*+1,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})=(H^{*,*}[b_{1},b_{2},\ldots])^{2*+1,*}=0
. Thus, using Remark
5.3
, for each even partition
\omega
we can define a map of left
A^{**}
-modules

\displaystyle\Phi_{\omega}:M_{B}u_{\omega}

\displaystyle\to H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot u_{\omega}

\displaystyle\mapsto\alpha(u_{\omega}).

Definition 5.5
.

We say that a partition
\omega=(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{s})
is
\ell
-adic
if at least one of its terms
\omega_{r}
is of the form
\omega_{r}=\ell^{m}-1
, for some
m\geq 1
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
P
be the set of all even partitions
\omega
that are not
\ell
-adic. Then the map

\Phi\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}\Phi_{\omega}:\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B}u_{\omega}\to H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})

is an isomorphism of left
A^{**}
-modules.

Before proving Lemma
5.6
, we need to review the analogous statement for
{\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
, studied in
[
27
, Chapter 1]
.

Let
H^{*}(-)
denote the mod
\ell
singular cohomology
H^{*}(-,\mathbb{Z}/\ell)
. We take
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}:={\operatorname{Sp}}_{2r}({\mathbb{C}})
, with the classical topology. This gives the classifying space
{\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}
, as well as the tautological rank
2r
symplectic bundle
E^{{\operatorname{Sp}},{\operatorname{top}}}_{2r}\to{\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}
and the Thom space
{\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}\coloneqq{\operatorname{Th}}(E^{{\operatorname{Sp}},{\operatorname{top}}}_{2r})
.
{\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
is the colimit of the shifted suspension spectra
\Sigma^{-4r}_{S^{1}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{S_{1}}{\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}
, and the cohomology
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
is the limit of the cohomologies
H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, for each fixed degree
*
, which is eventually constant. The cohomology algebra
H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, described for instance in
[
5
]
, is the polynomial algebra over
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
with generators the mod
\ell
quaternionic Borel classes
b_{j}^{\operatorname{top}}(E^{{\operatorname{Sp}},{\operatorname{top}}}_{2r})\in H^{4j}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, for
j=1,\ldots,r.
Novikov
[
27
, Lemma 3]
showed that the pullback by the zero section
z_{2r}^{{\operatorname{top}}*}:H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})\to H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
is injective, with image the ideal generated by the top Borel class
b_{r}^{\operatorname{top}}
. Writing
{\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}
as
{\operatorname*{colim}}_{r}{\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}
gives the isomorphism
H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong\lim_{r}H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, and similarly
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong\lim_{r}H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
. Together with the Thom isomorphisms
H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, this gives the commutative diagram of isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{r}H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{r}H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Under the isomorphism
H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
, we can write
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell[b_{1}^{\operatorname{top}},b_{2}^{\operatorname{top}},\ldots].

The action on
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
is induced by the action on
H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
for
r\gg 0
, thus by the action on
H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
via
z_{2r}^{{\operatorname{top}}*}
. Let us note that this does not identify with the action of
A^{\operatorname{top}}
on
H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
via the above isomorphism
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
. We can approximate
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
as
A^{\operatorname{top}}
-module by
H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)^{\operatorname{top}})
, with
{\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)^{\operatorname{top}}\coloneqq{\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)({\mathbb{C}})
with the classical topology, and similarly, we can approximate
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
as
A^{\operatorname{top}}
-module by
H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)^{\operatorname{top}})
. Explicitly,
b_{j}^{\operatorname{top}}\in H^{4j}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}_{2r})
corresponds to the elementary symmetric polynomial
\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{j}}t_{i_{i}}^{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot t_{i_{j}}^{2}
in
t_{1}^{2},\ldots,t_{r}^{2}
, and to each even partition
\omega=(2q_{1},\ldots,2q_{s})
, we can assign the element

v^{(r)}_{\omega}\coloneqq\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{s}}t_{i_{1}}^{2q_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot t_{i_{s}}^{2q_{s}}\in H^{2|\omega|}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}}).

The
v^{(r)}_{\omega}
are compatible in
r
, giving the elements
v_{\omega}\in H^{2|\omega|}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
, and we let
u_{\omega}\in H^{2|\omega|}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
be the element corresponding to
v_{\omega}
via the isomorphism
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\cong H^{*}({\operatorname{BSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
. Via the Thom isomorphism,
v^{(r)}_{\omega}
maps to an element
v_{\omega}^{(r)^{\prime}}\in H^{*+4r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
, and then
z_{2r}^{*}v_{\omega}^{(r)^{\prime}}=v^{(r)}_{\omega}\cdot b_{r}^{\operatorname{top}}\in H^{2|\omega|+4r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
. Dropping the
(r)
from the notation, we see that the action of the Steenrod algebra on
u_{\omega}\in H^{2|\omega|}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})
is given by the action on
v_{\omega}\cdot b_{r}^{\operatorname{top}}\in H^{2|\omega|+4r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}^{\operatorname{top}})
for
r\gg 0
.

The natural product
\mu_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}}:{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}\to{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}
on
{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}
is induced by the embeddings
{\operatorname{Sp}}(m)\times{\operatorname{Sp}}(n)\subset{\operatorname{Sp}}(m+n)
. Together with the Künneth formula,
\mu_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}}
gives rise to the “diagonal map”

\delta^{\operatorname{top}}:=\mu_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}}^{*}:H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\to H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\operatorname{top}}).

By
[
27
, Lemma 7]
the diagonal morphism on the generators
u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
has the following form:

(5.5)

\delta^{\operatorname{top}}(u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}})=\sum_{{(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=\omega}\atop{\omega_{1}\neq\omega_{2}}}(u_{\omega_{1}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\otimes u_{\omega_{2}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}+u_{\omega_{2}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\otimes u_{\omega_{1}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})+\sum_{(\omega_{1},\omega_{1})=\omega}(u_{\omega_{1}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\otimes u_{\omega_{1}}^{{\operatorname{top}}}).

As a module over
A^{{\operatorname{top}}}
,
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
is the direct sum of modules
M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
over even non-
\ell
-adic partitions
\omega
(this follows by
[
27
, Lemma 4]
). In other words, the map

\Phi^{{\operatorname{top}}}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}\Phi_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}:\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}\to H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})

where
\Phi_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
is defined in the same way as we defined
\Phi_{\omega}
, is an isomorphism of graded left
A^{{\operatorname{top}}}
-modules.

Proof of Lemma
5.6
.

By
[
44
, Chapter 6, Section 1]
, the
P_{{\operatorname{top}}}^{i}
and
\beta_{{\operatorname{top}}}
satisfy the Adem relations and
\beta_{{\operatorname{top}}}^{2}=0
, and these relations determine
A^{{\operatorname{top}}}
as
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-algebra. The same is true for the
P^{i}
and
\beta
by
[
18
, Theorem 5.1]
. Hence, there is a unique ring homomorphism
\theta:A^{{\operatorname{top}}}\to A^{**}
defined by sending
P_{{\operatorname{top}}}^{i}
to
P^{i}
and
\beta_{{\operatorname{top}}}
to
\beta
. In particular, by
[
22
, Lemma 4.1]
, if we let
A^{*,*}_{0}
be the
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-subalgebra of
A^{*,*}
generated by
\beta
and the
P^{i}
,
\theta
induces an isomorphism of rings
A^{{\operatorname{top}}}\to A^{*,*}_{0}
, and the
H^{*,*}
-linear extension of
\theta

{\operatorname{id}}_{H^{*,*}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}\theta:H^{*,*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}A^{{\operatorname{top}}}\to A^{*,*}

is an isomorphism of left
H^{*,*}
-modules. We similarly define
M_{B0}
as the
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-subalgebra of
M_{B}
generated by the
P^{i}
.

The action of
A^{*,*}
on
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
restricts to an action of
A^{*,*}_{0}
on
H^{0,0}[b_{1},b_{2},\ldots]=\mathbb{Z}/\ell[b_{1},b_{2},\ldots]
where
\beta
acts trivially. We denote by
H_{0}^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
the bigraded ring
\mathbb{Z}/\ell[b_{1},b_{2},\ldots]
. The map
\Phi_{\omega}
of
A^{*,*}
-modules induces then a map
\Phi_{\omega}^{0}
of
A_{0}^{*,*}
-modules, and we finally get a map

\Phi^{0}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}\Phi_{\omega}^{0}:\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B0}u_{\omega}\to H^{*,*}_{0}({\operatorname{MSp}}).

Let us now note that, as left
H^{*,*}
-modules,
M_{B}
is isomorphic to
H^{*,*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}M_{B0}
, and
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is isomorphic to
H^{*,*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell}H^{*,*}_{0}({\operatorname{MSp}})
. In particular, we have
\Phi_{\omega}={\operatorname{id}}_{H^{*,*}}\otimes\Phi_{\omega}^{0}
and
\Phi={\operatorname{id}}_{H^{*,*}}\otimes\Phi^{0}
as homomorphisms of
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-vector spaces. Thus, if
\Phi^{0}
is an isomorphism of
A^{*,*}_{0}
-modules,
\Phi
will be an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-vector spaces, and then also an isomorphism of left
A^{*,*}
-modules. It is then enough to prove that
\Phi^{0}
is an isomorphism of
A^{*,*}_{0}
-modules.

Let
\rho:H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})\to H^{*,*}_{0}({\operatorname{MSp}})
be the map defined by taking
b_{n}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
to
b_{n}
. It is immediate that
\rho
is an isomorphism of graded rings. We have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B0}u_{\omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{**}_{0}({\operatorname{MSp}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{{\operatorname{top}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{0}}

where
\theta^{\prime}
is the map induced by
\theta
. If
\rho
is also an isomorphism of left
A^{*,*}_{0}
-modules (using
A^{*,*}_{0}\cong A^{{\operatorname{top}}}
to define the module structure on
H^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
), all maps in the diagram except
\Phi^{0}
are isomorphisms of left
A^{*,*}_{0}
-modules, then, so is
\Phi^{0}
. We now prove that that
\rho
is an isomorphism of left
A^{*,*}_{0}
-modules.

Since the action of
\beta
is trivial, it is enough to show that
\rho
preserves the action of the power operations, that is,
\rho(P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}(b_{n}^{{\operatorname{top}}}))=P^{i}(b_{n})
for all
i
.

For each positive integer
r
, the action of
P^{i}
on
\mathbb{Z}/\ell[b_{1},\ldots,b_{r}]\subset H^{*,*}_{0}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is induced by the action on
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n))
as follows. As in the topological case, the canonical map
\Sigma_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{-2r}\Sigma^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}{\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r}\to{\operatorname{MSp}}
induces an isomorphism
H^{a,b}({\operatorname{MSp}})\cong H^{a+4r,b+2r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{r})
for
r\gg 0
, namely for
r
bigger than a certain positive integer
r_{0}
depending on
(a,b)
. By Proposition
2.67
(2), the restriction by the zero section
z_{2r}^{*}:H^{*+4r,*+2r}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{2r})\to H^{*+4r,*+2r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{r})
is injective, with image isomorphic to the ideal generated by the mod-
\ell
Borel class
b_{r}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2r})
. Similarly, we have an isomorphism
{\operatorname{BSp}}_{2r}\simeq{\operatorname*{colim}}_{n}{\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)
, in
{\mathcal{H}}(k)
, giving the approximation of
H^{*+4r,*+2r}({\operatorname{BSp}}_{r})
by
H^{*+4r,*+2r}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n))
for
n\gg 0
, and we can identify
H^{a,b}({\operatorname{MSp}})
with the bidegree
(a+4r,a+2r)
component of the ideal in
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n))
generated by the mod
\ell
Borel class
b_{r}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r,n})
, for
r,n\gg 0
. As the motivic Steenrod operations are
\Sigma^{a,b}
-stable, it follows that this identification of
H^{a,b}({\operatorname{MSp}})
with
H^{a+4r,b+2r}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n))
for
r,n\gg 0
is compatible with the respective
A^{*,*}
-module structures.

Thus,
P^{i}(b_{j})\in H^{4(i+j),2(i+j)}({\operatorname{MSp}})
corresponds to

P^{i}(b_{j}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r,n})\cdot b_{r}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r,n}))\in H^{4(i+j+r),2(i+j+r)}({\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)),\;\;r,n\gg 0.

Let
\text{HFlag}(1^{r};n)
be the complete quaternionic flag variety associated to
{\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)
as in
[
33
, §3, p. 147]
. By the quaternionic splitting principle
[
33
, Theorem 10.1]
, the pullback of
E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2r,2n}
by the map
\text{HFlag}(1^{r};n)\to{\operatorname{HGr}}(r,n)
splits as the orthogonal direct sum of the
r
universal rank
2
symplectic subbundles
(\mathcal{U}_{2}^{(1)},\phi_{2}^{(1)})\perp\ldots\perp(\mathcal{U}_{2}^{(r)},\phi_{2}^{(r)})
, and the cohomology pullback maps injectively the Borel classes
b_{j}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2r,2n})
to the elementary symmetric polynomials
e_{j}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1},\ldots,\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r})
in the Borel roots
\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m}\coloneqq b_{1}(\mathcal{U}_{2}^{(m)},\phi_{2}^{(m)})\in H^{4,2}(\text{HFlag}(1^{r};n))
. Then
P^{i}(b_{j}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r,n})\cdot b_{r}(E^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r,n}))
is determined by
P^{i}((e_{j}\cdot e_{r})(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1},\ldots,\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}))
. Moreover, by the Cartan formula
[
49
, Proposition 9.7]
,
P^{i}((e_{j}\cdot e_{r})(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1},\ldots,\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}))
is completely determined by the values
P^{s}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})
for
s=0,1,\ldots,i
, but those values are in turn completely determined by the properties of reduced power operations, as follows.

•

Since
P^{0}={\operatorname{id}}
by Lemma
5.2
(1),
P^{0}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})=\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m}
.

•

P^{1}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})=0
by looking at the degree and at the presentation of
H^{**}(\text{HFlag}(1^{r};n))
given by
[
33
, Theorem 11.1]
.

•

Since
\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m}
has bidegree
(2\cdot 2,2)
,
P^{2}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})=(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})^{\ell}
by Lemma
5.2
(2).

•

P^{s}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{m})=0
for all
s\geq 3
by Lemma
5.2
(3).

The analogous result works for the topological case. Namely, the same argument shows that
P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}(b_{j}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
is determined by the values
P^{s}_{{\operatorname{top}}}(\xi_{m}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
for
s=0,1,\ldots,i
, with
\xi_{m}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
the topological Borel roots of the complex symplectic flag manifold. For those values, the same expressions as before are valid, by the axioms of the power operations
P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}
(see for instance
[
44
, Chapter VI]
).

Thus, if we express
P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}(b_{n}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
as a polynomial
p
in the variables
e^{\operatorname{top}}_{j}=e_{j}(\xi_{1}^{{\operatorname{top}}},\ldots,\xi_{r}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
, the same polynomial
p
in the variables
e_{j}=e_{j}(\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1},\ldots,\xi^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r})
will give an expression for
P^{i}(b_{n})
, and thus:

\rho(P^{i}_{{\operatorname{top}}}(b_{n}^{{\operatorname{top}}}))=\rho(p(b_{1}^{{\operatorname{top}}},b_{2}^{{\operatorname{top}}},\ldots))=p(b_{1},b_{2},\ldots)=P^{i}(b_{n}).

∎

Because of the decomposition of
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
given by Lemma
5.6
, the algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
is generated by the algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(M_{B},H^{*,*})
and the dual elements of the cohomology classes
u_{\omega}
. The algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(M_{B},H^{*,*})
is completely studied in
[
22
, Section 5.1]
. In particular, we have the following computation.

Lemma 5.7
.

1.
[
22
, Lemma 5.1 and Lemma 5.4]
There is an isomorphism of trigraded
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-algebras

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(M_{B},H^{*,*})\simeq{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*}).

2.
[
22
, Lemma 5.2]
We have

•

\underline{t>2u}
:
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}^{s,(t-s,u)}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*})=0
.

•

\underline{t=2u}
:
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*})
is a polynomial
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-algebra in generators
\{h_{r}^{\prime}\}_{r\geq 0}
, with
{\operatorname{deg}}(h_{r}^{\prime})=(1,(1-2\ell^{r},1-\ell^{r}))
.

•

\underline{t<2u}
:
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}^{0,(2u-1,u)}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*})
is
H^{1,1}
if
u=1
, and
0
otherwise, and the product map

H^{1,1}\otimes\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*})\to{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}^{s,(2u-s+1,u+1)}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell,H^{*,*})

is surjective.

Now, let us define, for all even non-
\ell
-adic partitions
\omega
, elements
z_{(\omega)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-2|\omega|,-|\omega|)}
characterized by the relations
(z_{\omega},u_{\omega^{\prime}})=\delta_{\omega,\omega^{\prime}}
, where the pairing
(z_{\omega},u_{\omega^{\prime}})
is
z_{\omega}(u_{\omega^{\prime}})\in H^{*,*}
. Let us note that the diagonal map
\delta^{\operatorname{top}}
described above can be read in
\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B}^{{\operatorname{top}}}u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}}
through
\Phi^{{\operatorname{top}}}
, and it induces, via
\theta^{\prime}
and
\rho
, the diagonal map
\delta
of
\bigoplus_{\omega\in P}M_{B}u_{\omega}
. The expression (
5.5
) for
\delta^{{\operatorname{top}}}(u_{\omega}^{{\operatorname{top}}})
yields the analogous expression for
\delta(u_{\omega})
through
\rho
. Then, via the multiplication map on
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
induced by
\rho
, we see that the pairing
(z_{\omega_{1}}\cdot z_{\omega_{2}},u_{\omega})
can be computed by taking
(z_{\omega_{1}}\otimes z_{\omega_{2}},\delta(u_{\omega}))
and turning
\otimes
into products in
H^{*,*}
.

Now we write
(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
for the concatenation: namely
((\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}),(\omega^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\omega^{\prime}_{s}))
is the sequence
(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\omega^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\omega^{\prime}_{s})
, reordered to be non-increasing. It is then easy to see that
(z_{\omega_{1}}\otimes z_{\omega_{2}},\delta(u_{\omega}))
is
1
if
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
, and
0
otherwise, which gives
z_{\omega_{1}}\cdot z_{\omega_{2}}=z_{(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})}
.

Lemma
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
implies that the
H^{*,*}
-subalgebra

\oplus_{k\geq 0}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-4k,-2k)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})

of
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{*,(*,*)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
is the free
H^{*,*}
-module on the elements
z_{\omega}
,
\omega\in P
, the only indecomposable elements are the elements
z_{(2k)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-4k,-2k)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
corresponding to the trivial partitions
\omega=(2k)
, with
2k
not of the form
\ell^{i}-1
, and the subalgebra generated by the

z_{(2k)}
is the polynomial algebra over
H^{*,*}
on the
z_{(2k)}
.

Taking
k=0
, the element
z_{(0)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(0,0)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
dual to
u_{(0)}
is the identity in the
{\operatorname{Ext}}
-algebra, and the summand
\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,s)}(M_{B}u_{(0)},H^{*,*})

of
\oplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,s)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
is the polynomial algebra on generators
\{h_{r}^{\prime}\}_{r\geq 0}
corresponding to the polynomial generators
\{h_{r}^{\prime}\}_{r\geq 0}
described in Lemma
5.7
. Moreover, we have
h_{r_{1}}^{\prime}z_{\omega_{1}}\cdot h_{r_{2}}^{\prime}z_{\omega_{2}}=h_{r_{1}}^{\prime}h_{r_{2}}^{\prime}z_{(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})}
, and for each partition
\omega\in P
, the assignment
x\mapsto x\cdot z_{\omega}
gives an isomorphism

\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,s)}(M_{B}u_{(0)},H^{*,*})\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,s)}(M_{B}u_{\omega},H^{*,*}).

Putting all the information from Lemma
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
together with the product structure on
\{z_{\omega}\}
described above, we get the following.

Proposition 5.8
.

The
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
-algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
has the following presentation:

•

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})=0
if
t>2u
.

•

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
is polynomial in the generators:

–

1\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(0,0)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
;

–

z_{(2k)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-4k,-2k)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
, for
k\geq 1
, and
2k\neq\ell^{i}-1\;\forall\;i\geq 0
;

–

h_{r}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{1,(1-2\ell^{r},1-\ell^{r})}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
, for
r\geq 0
.

•

{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(2u-1,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{**})
is
H^{1,1}
if
u=1
, and
0
otherwise, and the product map

H^{1,1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})=\\
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(1,1)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\to\\
\bigoplus_{s,u}{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s+1,u+1)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})

is surjective.

Remark 5.9
.

Comparing the descriptions of
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
and
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
given by Lemma
5.6
and Proposition
5.8
with their analogues for
{\operatorname{MGL}}
given by
[
22
, Lemma 5.9 and Proposition 5.7]
, we see that
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is a summand of
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}})
as
A^{*,*}
-modules,
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
is the corresponding
H^{*,*}
-summand of
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
, and
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
is the polynomial algebra over
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
on generators
z_{(k)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-2k,-k)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
for
k
odd. All these comparison maps are induced by the map
\Phi:{\operatorname{MSp}}\to{\operatorname{MGL}}
of Remark
2.66
.

5.3.
The Adams spectral sequence

We denote by
\overline{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}
the homotopy cofiber of the unit map
\epsilon_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}:1_{k}\to H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
. For
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
, letting
{\mathbb{E}}_{s}\coloneqq\overline{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge s}\wedge{\mathbb{E}}
and
W_{s}({\mathbb{E}})\coloneqq H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\wedge{\mathbb{E}}_{s}
gives the standard
Adams tower
of
{\mathbb{E}}

\{{\mathbb{E}}_{s},W_{s}({\mathbb{E}})\}_{s\geq 0}
(see for example
[
22
, Section 6.1]
).

Definition 5.10
.

(1)

The
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-nilpotent completion
of
{\mathbb{E}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{E}}^{\wedge}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}
, is the homotopy limit of the Adams tower
\{{\mathbb{E}}_{s},W_{s}({\mathbb{E}})\}_{s\geq 0}
.

(2)

The (Mod-
\ell
)
Adams spectral sequence for
{\mathbb{E}}
is the spectral sequence associated to the Adams tower of
{\mathbb{E}}
. Explicitly, it is given by

E_{1}^{s,t,u}\coloneqq W_{s}({\mathbb{E}})^{t,u}\Rightarrow({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u},

with filtration degree
s
, cohomological bidegree
(t,u)
, and differentials
d_{r}^{s,t,u}:E_{r}^{s,t,u}\to E_{r}^{s+r,t+1,u}
.

We need to introduce the following technical condition.

Definition 5.11
.

For
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
, we say that
{\mathbb{E}}
is a
motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
if there exist
p,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and a set of bidegrees
\{p_{\alpha},q_{\alpha}\}{{}_{\alpha}\in S}
such that

\Sigma^{p,q}{\mathbb{E}}\simeq\oplus_{\alpha\in S}\Sigma^{p_{\alpha},q_{\alpha}}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell,

with the bidegrees
(p_{\alpha},q_{\alpha})
satisfying the conditions:

(1)

p_{\alpha}\geq 2q_{\alpha}\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in S
,

(2)

For all
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
q_{\alpha}\leq m
for almost all
\alpha
.

Proposition 5.12

(
[
10
]
, Remark 6.11 and Proposition 6.1)
.

Let us suppose that
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
is a cellular spectrum and
W_{s}({\mathbb{E}})
is a motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
for all
s
. Then the
E_{2}
-page of the Adams spectral sequence for
{\mathbb{E}}
has the form

E_{2}^{s,t,u}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\mathbb{E}}),H^{*,*}).

If in addition, fixed
(t,u)
, we have
\lim_{r}^{1}E_{r}^{s,t,u}({\mathbb{E}})=0
for all
s
, the spectral sequence converges completely to
({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}
. That is, the natural map
({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}\to\lim_{s}K_{s}({\mathbb{E}})^{t,u}
is an isomorphism, the associated filtration

F_{s}({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}\coloneqq\ker(({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}\to K_{s-1}({\mathbb{E}})^{t,u})\subset({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}

is exhaustive, and the natural map

F_{s}({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}/F_{s+1}({\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u}\to E_{\infty}^{s,t,u}({\mathbb{E}})

is an isomorphism for all
s\geq 0
.

See
[
22
, Proposition 6.6]
for a proof (instead of ordinary cellularity they use the weaker hypothesis of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-cellularity, but we don’t need it).

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a cellular ring spectrum with multiplication
\mu_{{\mathcal{E}}}
, such that each
W_{s}({\mathcal{E}})
is a motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
. Let us also suppose that

H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}})
is flat over
H^{*,*}
and the canonical map
H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}})\otimes_{H^{*,*}}H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}})\to H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}}\wedge{\mathcal{E}})
is an isomorphism. Then, the Adams spectral sequence for
{\mathcal{E}}
has a multiplicative structure induced by
\mu_{\mathcal{E}}
and compatible with the multiplicative structure on
({\mathcal{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{*,*}
. Also, the product on the
E_{2}
-terms induced by the multiplicative structure on the spectral sequence agrees with the associative
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra structure on
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}}),H^{*,*})
given as in (
5.3
) and the isomorphism

E_{2}^{s,t,u}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\mathcal{E}}),H^{*,*})

given by Proposition
5.12
.

See
[
22
, Proposition 6.7]
for a proof.

Let us now recall that we have the unstable Hopf map
\eta_{u}:{\mathbb{A}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
defined by
(x,y)\mapsto[x:y]
. We also have
{\mathbb{A}}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\wedge{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
in
{\mathcal{H}}_{\bullet}(k)
by
[
26
, §3 Example 2.20]
. Then we consider the
stable Hopf map
as the map
\eta:=\Sigma^{-2,-1}\Sigma^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\eta_{u}:\Sigma^{1,1}1_{k}\to 1_{k}
.

Definition 5.14
.

We denote by
\mathbb{S}_{k}/\eta^{n}
the homotopy cofiber of
\eta^{n}:\Sigma^{n,n}1_{k}\to 1_{k}
. The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n+1,n+1}1_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{k}/\eta^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n,n}1_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{k}/\eta^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{n}}

induces the map
1_{k}/\eta^{n+1}\to 1_{k}/\eta^{n}
on the cofibers. For
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
we then get a tower
\{1/\eta^{n}\wedge{\mathbb{E}}\}_{n>0}
over
1/\eta\wedge{\mathbb{E}}
. We call the
\eta
-completion of
{\mathbb{E}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}^{\wedge}
, the homotopy limit of this tower.

Definition 5.15
.

For
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we recall that the Moore spectrum
S{\mathbb{Z}}/m
is the cofiber of the map
\mathbb{S}_{k}\xrightarrow{\times m}\mathbb{S}_{k}
in
{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
. For
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
, the
\ell
-adic completion of
{\mathbb{E}}
, denoted by
{\mathbb{E}}_{\ell}^{\wedge}
, is the homotopy limit of the tower
\{S{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell^{n}\wedge{\mathbb{E}}\}_{n\geq 0}
over
S{\mathbb{Z}}\wedge{\mathbb{E}}
.

As a matter of notation, we will use the shorthand
{\mathbb{E}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell}\coloneqq({\mathbb{E}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})_{\ell}^{\wedge}
for the
(\eta,\ell)
-completion of
{\mathbb{E}}
.

In
[
23
]
, Mantovani proved a comparison between the
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-nilpotent completion and the
(\eta,\ell)
-completion of a spectrum
{\mathbb{E}}
, with the only hypothesis of connectedness of
{\mathbb{E}}
, generalizing some results showed in
[
19
]
(in particular the first claim of
[
19
, Theorem 1]
). In order to define connectedness, let us recall that for any motivic spectrum
{\mathbb{E}}
, we have a bigraded homotopy sheaf
\pi_{n,m}({\mathbb{E}})
given by the Nisnevich sheafification of the presheaf
U\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{-n,-m}(U)
on
{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
. One then uses the notations
\pi_{p}({\mathbb{E}})_{q}\coloneqq\pi_{p-q,-q}({\mathbb{E}})
and
\pi_{p}({\mathbb{E}})\coloneqq\oplus_{q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\pi_{p}({\mathbb{E}})_{q}
.

Definition 5.16
.

A motivic spectrum
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
is called
r
-connected
if for all
p\leq r
,
\pi_{p}({\mathbb{E}})=0
, and it is called
connected
if it is
r
-connected for some integer
r
.

Combining some of the main results of
[
23
]
, one gets the following.

Theorem 5.17

(
[
23
]
, Theorem 4.3.5, Example 5.2, Theorem 7.3.4)
.

If
{\mathbb{E}}\in{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
is a connected motivic spectrum, then
{\mathbb{E}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge}\simeq{\mathbb{E}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge}
.

Proposition 5.18
.

{\operatorname{MSp}}
is a
(-1)
-connected spectrum.

Proof.

The argument is basically the same as in
[
22
, Corollary 2.3]
, replacing
{\operatorname{Gr}}(n,n+m)
with
{\operatorname{HGr}}(n,np)
, the tautological bundle over
{\operatorname{Gr}}(n,n+m)
with
E_{n,np}^{\operatorname{Sp}}
and
\Sigma^{-2n-n}
-suspensions with
\Sigma^{-4n-2n}
-suspensions. The symplectic structure on
E_{n,np}^{\operatorname{Sp}}
does not play any role here.
∎

Theorem
5.17
and Proposition
5.18
give the following.

Corollary 5.19
.

In
{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
, we have
{\operatorname{MSp}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge}\simeq{\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge}
.

We can finally compute the Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
.

Proposition 5.20
.

The Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
is of the form

E_{2}^{s,t,u}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})\Rightarrow({\operatorname{MSp}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u},

with differentials
d_{r}^{s,t,u}:E_{r}^{s,t,u}\to E_{r}^{s+r,t+1,u}
.

Proof.

We need to verify the two conditions of Proposition
5.12
for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
.

The fact that
{\operatorname{MSp}}
is cellular is Corollary
4.8
. Therefore, we just need to show that
W_{s}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is a motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
for all
s
.

Letting
W_{s}\coloneqq H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\wedge\overline{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge s}
, we have
W_{s}({\operatorname{MSp}})=W_{s}\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}=W_{s}\wedge_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}
. By Proposition
4.9
and Remark
4.11
, we can write

H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell\wedge{\operatorname{MSp}}\simeq\oplus_{\alpha\geq 0}\Sigma^{4n_{\alpha},2n_{\alpha}}H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell,

for some non-negative integers
n_{\alpha}
such that, for all
n
, there are only finitely many indices
\alpha
with
n_{\alpha}=n
. Moreover, by
[
22
, Lemma 6.8]
, we can write

W_{s}=\oplus_{(p,q)\in S_{s}}\Sigma^{p,q}(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)^{r_{p,q}},

where
S_{s}=\{(p,q)\mid p+s\geq 2q\geq 0\}
, and for each
q
,
r_{p,q}=0
for almost all
p
. We note that
(4n_{\alpha},2n_{\alpha})\in S_{s}
, and we conclude that we can write

W_{s}({\operatorname{MSp}})\simeq\oplus_{(p,q)\in S_{s}}\Sigma^{p,q}(H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)^{n_{p,q}},

where, again,
S_{s}=\{(p,q)\mid p+s\geq 2q\geq 0\}
, and for each
q
,
n_{p,q}=0
for almost all
p
. In particular,
W_{s}({\operatorname{MSp}})
is a motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
for all
s
.
∎

Proposition 5.21
.

In the Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
, if we let
E_{r}^{t,u}\coloneqq\oplus_{s}E_{r}^{s,t,u}
, we have
E_{2}^{2u,u}\simeq E_{\infty}^{2u,u}
, and the spectral sequence converges completely to
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{2u,u}
.

Proof.

For all
s,t,u
, we have
E_{2}^{s,t,u}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
by Proposition
5.20
. In particular, from Proposition
5.8
(1), we get
E_{2}^{2u+1,u}=0
. Hence,
d_{r}^{2u,u}
vanishes for all
r\geq 2
. By Proposition
5.8
(2),
E_{2}^{r,2,1}=0
for all
r\geq 2
. Therefore,
d_{r}^{0,1,1}
will vanish for all
r\geq 2
. Finally, by Proposition
5.8
(3),
E_{2}^{0,1,1}=H^{1,1}
.

By Proposition
5.13
, the product on
E_{2}
given by the multiplicative structure on the spectral sequence is the product of the
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}}),H^{*,*})
of Proposition
5.8
. In particular, the product

E_{2}^{0,1,1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}\bigoplus_{s}E_{2}^{s,2u-2,u-1}\to\bigoplus_{s}E_{2}^{s,2u-1,u}

is surjective. Thus, from the vanishing of differentials
d_{r}^{0,1,1}
and
d_{r}^{2u,u}
, we obtain the vanishing of differentials
d_{r}^{2u-1,u}
.

Now, since the differentials
d_{r}^{2u,u}
and
d_{r}^{2u-1,u}
are zero for
r\geq 2
, we have that
E_{r+1}^{2u,u}=E_{r}^{2u,u}
for
r\geq 2
, from which it follows that
E_{2}^{2u,u}=E_{\infty}^{2u,u}
and
\lim_{r}^{1}E_{r}^{s,2u,u}=0
. Hence, by Proposition
5.12
, the spectral sequence converges completely to
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{2u,u}
. Finally, by Corollary
5.19
, we get
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{2u,u}\cong({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{2u,u}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

6.
Constructing symplectic bordism classes

Proposition
2.60
says that the symplectic Thom class construction descends to
K^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{0}(X)
. Thus, for
(v,\omega)\in K^{\operatorname{Sp}}_{0}(X)
a virtual symplectic vector bundle on
X
with virtual rank
2r
, we have the isomorphism

{\operatorname{th}}^{{\operatorname{Sp}},f}_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}(v,\omega):\Sigma^{-4r,-2r}\Sigma^{v}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}.

We now want to define a symplectic Thom isomorphism for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
-cohomology.

Definition 6.1

(Symplectic Thom Isomorphism)
.

Let
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
and
(v,\omega)\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)
of virtual rank
2r
. We define a natural isomorphism

{\operatorname{Th}}_{\operatorname{MSp}}(v,\omega):{\operatorname{MSp}}^{a,b}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4r+a,2r+b}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})

as the composition

{\operatorname{MSp}}^{a,b}(X)=[p_{\#}1_{X},\Sigma^{a,b}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\simeq[1_{X},p_{X}^{*}\Sigma^{a,b}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\Sigma^{4r+a,2r+b}\Sigma^{-v}{\operatorname{th}}^{{\operatorname{Sp}},f}_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}(v,\omega)_{*}}\\
[1_{X},\Sigma^{-v}p_{X}^{*}\Sigma^{4r+a,2r+b}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\xrightarrow[\sim]{(1)}[\Sigma^{v}1_{X},p_{X}^{*}\Sigma^{4r+a,2r+b}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\\
\xrightarrow[\sim]{(2)}[p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X},\Sigma^{4r+a,2r+b}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(k)}={\operatorname{MSp}}^{4r+a,2r+b}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X}),

where
(1)
is given by applying
\Sigma^{v}:{\operatorname{SH}}(X)\to{\operatorname{SH}}(X)
and the
(2)
is given by the adjunction
p_{X\#}\dashv p_{X}^{*}
.

Definition 6.2
.

For
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
and
(v,\omega)\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)
of virtual rank
2r
, we define the
{\operatorname{MSp}}
-cohomology class

[(v,\omega)]_{\operatorname{MSp}}:={\operatorname{Th}}_{\operatorname{MSp}}(v,\omega)(1_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)})\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4r,2r}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X}).

In particular, for two symplectic vector bundles
(V,\omega)
,
(V^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})
on
X
of respective ranks
2r,2r^{\prime}
, we have the Thom isomorphism

{\operatorname{Th}}_{\operatorname{MSp}}((V,\omega)-(V^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})):{\operatorname{MSp}}^{a,b}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4(r-r^{\prime})+a,2(r-r^{\prime})+b}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{V-V^{\prime}}1_{X}),

and the
{\operatorname{MSp}}
class
[(V,\omega)-(V^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4(r-r^{\prime}),2(r-r^{\prime})}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{V-V^{\prime}}1_{X})
.

Remark 6.3
.

In Definition
6.2
, if the negative contribution is null, the class
[(V,\omega)]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}
is just the symplectic Thom class
{\operatorname{th}}_{\operatorname{Sp}}^{{\operatorname{MSp}}}(V,\omega)
. The notation
[-]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}
is indeed intended to be a generalization of the symplectic Thom class to virtual symplectic vector bundles.

Now, let us consider the graded abelian group
\oplus_{v\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})
. We make this into a

K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)
-graded ring by giving the product

{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})\times{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v^{\prime}),{\operatorname{rank}}(v^{\prime})}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v^{\prime}}1_{X})\\
\to{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v+v^{\prime}),{\operatorname{rank}}(v+v^{\prime})}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v+v^{\prime}}1_{X})

induced by the product maps

[\Sigma^{v}1_{X},\Sigma^{a,b}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\times[\Sigma^{v^{\prime}}1_{X},\Sigma^{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\\
\to[\Sigma^{v}1_{X}\wedge_{X}\Sigma^{v^{\prime}}1_{X},\Sigma^{a+a^{\prime},b+b^{\prime}}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}\wedge_{X}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)}\to[\Sigma^{v+v^{\prime}}1_{X},\Sigma^{a+a^{\prime},b+b^{\prime}}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}]_{{\operatorname{SH}}(X)},

where the last map is induced by the isomorphism
\Sigma^{v}1_{X}\wedge_{X}\Sigma^{v^{\prime}}1_{X}\simeq\Sigma^{v+v^{\prime}}1_{X}
and the multiplication map
\mu_{p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}}:p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}\wedge_{X}p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}\to p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}
. We denote this product by

\smile:\oplus_{v\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})\times\oplus_{v\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})\\
\to\oplus_{v\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X}).

Note that the unit
1_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)
acts as (left and right) unit for this product.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let us take
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
. Then sending
(v,\omega)\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)
to
[(v,\omega)]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X})
defines a multiplicative map

[-]_{\operatorname{MSp}}:K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)\to\oplus_{v\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(X)}{\operatorname{MSp}}^{2{\operatorname{rank}}(v),{\operatorname{rank}}(v)}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{X}).

That is,

[(v,\omega)+(v^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}=[(v^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\smile[(v^{\prime},\omega^{\prime})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}

and
[0]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
is the unit
1_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)
.

Proof.

The multiplicativity follows directly from the multiplicativity of the symplectic Thom classes, as expressed in Lemma
2.59
.

The class
[0]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
is the image of
1_{{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{0,0}(X)
under the symplectic Thom isomorphism
{\operatorname{th}}^{{\operatorname{Sp}},f}_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}(0):p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}\to p_{X}^{*}{\operatorname{MSp}}
, namely the identity map.
∎

6.1.
Cobordism classes from stable symplectic twists

We recall that a
symplectic variety

(X,\omega_{X})
is a variety
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
with a symplectic form
\omega_{X}
on the tangent bundle
T_{X}
. In particular, this gives a class

[-(T_{X},\omega_{X})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{-T_{X}}1_{X}),

with
2d={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}X
.

If
f:X\to Y
is a proper map in
{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
, and
X,Y
are symplectic, a symplectic version of Construction
2.30
(2) gives maps
f_{*}:{\operatorname{MSp}}^{a,b}(p_{X!}(1_{X}))\to{\operatorname{MSp}}^{a,b}(p_{Y!}(1_{Y}))
through the symplectic Thom isomorphism. In particular, if
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
is proper symplectic of dimension
2d
, with structure map
p_{X}
, we have a map

p_{X*}:{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{-T_{X}}1_{X})\to{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}(1_{k})={\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}(k)={\operatorname{MSp}}_{4d,2d}(k),

and we can define the class

[X,-(T_{X},\omega_{X})]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}\coloneqq p_{X*}[-(T_{X},\omega_{X})]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}_{4d,2d}(k).

Remark 6.5
.

If
\Phi:{\operatorname{MSp}}\to{\operatorname{MGL}}
is the natural map of Remark
2.66
, one sees by construction that
\Phi_{*}[X,-(T_{X},\omega_{X})]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}=[X]_{{\operatorname{MGL}}}\in{\operatorname{MGL}}_{4d,2d}(k)
, where
[X]_{{\operatorname{MGL}}}
is the algebraic cobordism class of
X
defined by the
{\operatorname{GL}}
-orientation, see Definition
2.45
.

The standard non-trivial examples of symplectic varieties in algebraic geometry are not easy to handle, and it is not clear what their characteristic numbers
s_{d}
are. In order to construct generators of
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}
, we want to extend our symplectic Pontryagin-Thom construction to varieties with a stable symplectic twist. We then give the symplectic version of Definitions
2.32
,
2.33
.

Definition 6.6
.

Given
Y\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
, a
stable symplectic twist of
-T_{Y}
is a tuple
((v,\omega),\vartheta,m)
, where
(v,\omega)\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(Y)
is a virtual symplectic bundle and
\vartheta
is an isomorphism
\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{2m,m}\Sigma^{v}1_{Y}
in
{\operatorname{SH}}(Y)
.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
Y\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
with structure morphism
p_{Y}:Y\to{\operatorname{Spec}}k
proper of dimension
d_{Y}
and let
((v,\omega),\vartheta,m)
be a stable symplectic twist of
-T_{Y}
. Let
2r={\operatorname{rank}}(v)
. We write the element
[v,\omega]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4r,2r}(p_{X\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{Y})
as a map

[v,\omega]_{\operatorname{MSp}}:p_{Y\#}\Sigma^{v}1_{Y}\to\Sigma^{4r,2r}{\operatorname{MSp}}

in
{\operatorname{SH}}(k)
, and we define the class
[Y,(v,\omega),\vartheta,m]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{4r+2m,2r+m}(k)
as the composition

1_{k}\xrightarrow{p_{Y}^{\vee}}p_{Y\#}\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{p_{Y\#}\vartheta}p_{Y\#}\Sigma^{2m,m}\Sigma^{v}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\Sigma^{2m,m}[v,\omega]_{\operatorname{MSp}}}\Sigma^{2m+4r,m+2r}{\operatorname{MSp}}.

In particular, this gives a twisted symplectic analogue of Definition
2.45
. We want to construct symplectic bordism classes by using Ananyevskiy’s isomorphism (
3.1
).

Example 6.8

(Odd dimensional projective spaces)
.

Let
X=\mathbb{P}^{2n+1}
for a non-negative integer
n
. We have the Euler exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)^{\oplus 2n+2}\to T_{X}\to 0,

giving
\Sigma^{T_{X}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}}1_{X}\simeq\Sigma^{\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)^{2n+2}}1_{X}
. As in (
3.1
), we get the isomorphism in
{\operatorname{SH}}(X)

\Sigma^{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)^{n+1}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)^{n+1}}1_{X}\xrightarrow{\Sigma^{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)^{n+1}}({\operatorname{Anan}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1),\ldots,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)})}\\
\Sigma^{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)^{n+1}}(\Sigma^{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)^{n+1}}1_{X})\simeq\Sigma^{T_{X}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}1_{X}.

The bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)^{n+1}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}(-1)^{n+1}
is of the form
V\oplus V^{\vee}
, thus, as in Example
2.52
(2), it has a canonical symplectic structure given by the standard symplectic form.
Let us denote by
(W,\phi_{W})
be the symplectic vector bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)^{n+1}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}(-1)^{n+1},\phi_{2n+2})
. We then get the stable symplectic twist
(-(W,\phi_{W}),\vartheta,1)
of
-T_{X}
given by the isomorphism

\vartheta:=\Sigma^{-T_{X}-{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)^{n+1}}({\operatorname{Anan}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1),\ldots,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)}):\Sigma^{-T_{X}}1_{X}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{2,1}\Sigma^{-W}1_{X}.

Via Definition
6.7
, we obtain the twisted class

[{\mathbb{P}}^{2n+1},-(W,\phi_{W}),\vartheta,1]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4n-2,-2n-1}(k)={\operatorname{MSp}}_{4n+2,2n+1}(k).

The problem with the symplectic class of Example
6.8
is that its image through the motivic Hurewicz map
h_{H{\mathbb{Z}}}
lies in homological bidegree
(4n+2,2n+1)
, and since the motivic homology of
{\operatorname{MSp}}
is generated by elements of even weight, this class has trivial homology. We now refine this procedure to get classes with non-trivial homology. The construction is inspired by a topological analogue due to Stong
[
45
, Section 4]
.

Construction 6.9
.

We work over an infinite field
k
.
We take a direct product of an even number
2r
of odd dimensional projective spaces

X=\mathbb{P}^{2n_{1}+1}\times_{k}\ldots\times_{k}\mathbb{P}^{2n_{2r}+1}.

Let
\xi=\mathcal{O}(1,\ldots,1)
be the line bundle
p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)\otimes\ldots\otimes p_{2r}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)
over
X
, where
p_{j}
is the projection on the
j
-th component. The rank
2
vector bundle
\xi\oplus\xi
over
X
is globally generated. By Proposition
6.10
below, there exists a global section
s
of
\xi\oplus\xi
, such that its vanishing locus is a closed subvariety
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
of codimension
2
of
X
, smooth and proper over
k
. Let
\xi^{\prime}
denote the restriction
i^{*}\xi
. The normal bundle
N_{i}
over
Y
is
i^{*}(\xi\oplus\xi)=\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime}
. We get the short exact sequence

0\to T_{Y}\to i^{*}T_{X}\to N_{i}\to 0

of vector bundles over
Y
, which gives the canonical isomorphism
\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\cong\Sigma^{N_{i}-i^{*}T_{X}}1_{Y}
. Following Example
6.8
, we get symplectic bundles
(W_{j},\phi_{W_{j}})
on
{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{j}+1}
, and isomorphisms

\vartheta_{j}:\Sigma^{-T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{j}+1}}}1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{j}+1}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{2,1}\Sigma^{-W_{j}}1_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{j}+1}};\quad j=1,\ldots 2r,

inducing the isomorphism

\wedge_{j=1}^{2r}\vartheta_{j}:\Sigma^{-T_{X}}1_{X}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{4r,2r}\Sigma^{-\oplus_{j=1}^{2r}p_{j}^{*}W_{j}}1_{X}.

In addition, we have the Ananyevskiy isomorphism

\vartheta_{0}:=\Sigma^{\xi}{\operatorname{Anan}}_{\xi}:\Sigma^{N_{i}}1_{Y}=\Sigma^{\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee}}1_{Y}

and the standard symplectic bundle
(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee},\phi_{2})
, as in Example
2.52
(2) again. Putting this together gives the stable symplectic twist of
-T_{Y}

((\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee},\phi_{2})-\oplus_{i=1}^{2r}i^{*}p_{j}^{*}(W_{j},\phi_{W_{j}}),\vartheta_{Y},2r),

with
\vartheta_{Y}
being the isomorphism

\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\simeq\Sigma^{N_{i}-i^{*}T_{X}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\vartheta_{0}\wedge(\wedge_{j=1}^{2r}i^{*}\vartheta_{j})}\Sigma^{4r,2r}\Sigma^{(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee})-\sum_{j}i^{*}p_{j}^{*}W_{j}}1_{Y}.

Let
2n:={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}X=2r+2\sum_{j=1}^{2r}n_{j}
. So
{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}Y=2n-2
, and
{\operatorname{rank}}((\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee}-\sum_{j}i^{*}p_{j}^{*}W_{j})=-2n-2r+2
. Via Definition
6.7
, this gives us the twisted class

[Y,(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee},\phi_{2})-\sum_{j}i^{*}p_{j}^{*}(W_{j},\phi_{W_{j}}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4n+4,-2n+2}(k).

Proposition 6.10

(Variant of Bertini’s Theorem)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety over
k
, and let
L
be a very ample line bundle over
X
. Then there exists a Zariski open subset
U
of the projective space
\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(X,L))
on
H^{0}(X,L)
such that, for each section
s
in
U
, its vanishing locus
V(s)
defines a codimension one closed subscheme of
X\times_{k}k(s)
that is smooth over
k(s)
. In particular, if
k
is infinite, there exists a section
s\neq 0
in
H^{0}(X,L)
such that
V(s)
is a codimension one closed subscheme of
X
, smooth over
k
.

Proof.

Since
X
is projective, the
k
-vector space
H^{0}(X,L)
of global sections is finite-dimensional, and we have
{\mathbb{P}}(H^{0}(X,L))\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, with
N=h^{0}(X,L)-1
. Since
L
is very ample, there exists a closed immersion
i:X\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}(H^{0}(X,L))\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
such that
L
is the pullback
i^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)
of the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)\to{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, and the hyperplane sections of
X
are exactly the closed subschemes
Z(s)\hookrightarrow X
given by zero loci of global sections
s:X\to L
.

Let
d\coloneqq{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}X
. For each point
x\in X
, we consider the projective tangent space
\overline{T}_{(X,x)}
of
X
at the point
x
as a
d
-dimensional projective subspace
{\mathbb{P}}^{d}\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
. Let us consider the dual space
({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}
of
{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, defined as the space parametrizing the hyperplane divisors in
{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, which is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, and take the subset
W\subseteq X\times({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}
defined by

W\coloneqq\{(x,H)\in X\times({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}\mid\overline{T}_{(x,X)}\subseteq H\}.

It is easy to see that for any
d
-dimensional linear subspace
L\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{d}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
, the set of hyperplanes of
{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
containing
L
can be parametrized by the set of hyperplanes in a complementary linear subspace
L^{\prime}
, by taking
L^{\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N-d-1}
such that
L\cap L^{\prime}=\{0\}
. Therefore, for each point
x\in X
, the fibre
f^{-1}(x)\subset W
over
x
through
f:W\hookrightarrow X\times{\mathbb{P}}^{N}\xrightarrow{p_{1}}X
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{N-d-1}
, and
W\subset X\times({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}
is closed. We deduce that
W
has dimension
N-1<N
. In particular, the closed subset
p_{2}(W)\subset({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
is a proper closed subset. We can then consider the open complement
U\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}^{N}\setminus p_{2}(W)
of
{\mathbb{P}}^{N}
.

Let us now take a hyperplane
H\in U
. For every point
x\in X
, the intersection
H\cap\overline{T}_{X,x}
is a codimension
1
linear space in
\overline{T}_{X,x}
. This is true in particular for all
x\in X\cap H
, which means that
X\cap H
is smooth over the field of definition
k(H)
of
H
. We can then identify
({\mathbb{P}}^{N})^{*}
with
{\mathbb{P}}(H^{0}(X,L)
,
U
with an open subspace of
{\mathbb{P}}(H^{0}(X,L)
, and hyperplanes
H\in U
with sections
s
in
U
. We obtain that, for each
s
,
Z(s)
is a codimension
1
subscheme of
X\times_{k}k(s)
, smooth over
k(s)
.
∎

Definition 6.11
.

Let
X
and
Y
as in Construction
6.9
and let
d_{Y}:={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}Y
. Let us denote by

(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})
the virtual symplectic bundle
(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee},\phi_{2})-\sum_{j}i^{*}p_{j}^{*}(W_{j},\phi_{W_{j}})
, and let
\vartheta_{Y}:\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{4r,2r}\Sigma^{v_{Y}}1_{Y}
be the isomorphism described in Construction
6.9
. This gives the stable symplectic twist
((v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r)
of
-T_{Y}
, and the symplectic bordism class

[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-2d_{Y},-d_{Y}}(k)={\operatorname{MSp}}_{2d_{Y},d_{Y}}(k).

Since
\vartheta_{Y}
and
2r
are canonical, we can use the shorthand
[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}:=[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
.

We have then achieved the following:

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
k
be an infinite field. For every
X\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
given by a product of an even number of odd dimensional projective spaces, of total dimension
2d+2
, let
\xi
be the very ample invertible sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1,\ldots,1)
. Then there is a codimension
2
irreducible proper subvariety
Y
of
X
, smooth over
k
, given as the zero subscheme of a section of
\xi\oplus\xi
, that defines a class
[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}_{4d,2d}(k)
.

We now take care of the characteristic numbers
s_{(2d)}(Y)
(Definition
2.38
(4)).

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
Y,X
be as in Construction
6.9
, with
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
the closed immersion. We again denote
{\operatorname{dim}}_{k}X
by
2n
, and let us suppose that
X
is not of the form
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{2n-1}
. Let
2d\coloneqq 2n-2={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}Y
. Also, let
\alpha\coloneqq c_{1}(\xi)\in H\mathbb{Z}^{2,1}(X)
. Then

s_{(2d)}(Y)=(-2)\cdot{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(\alpha^{2d+2}).

Proof.

The short exact sequence

0\to T_{Y}\to i^{*}T_{X}\to N_{i}\to 0

gives
c_{(2d)}(T_{Y})+c_{(2d)}(N_{i})=c_{(2d)}(i^{*}T_{X})
, but since
X
is a product of varieties each of dimension less than
2d
, Lemma
2.39
(3)-(4) gives
c_{(2d)}(i^{*}T_{X})=i^{*}c_{(2d)}(T_{X})=0
. Then

c_{(2d)}(T_{Y})=-c_{(2d)}(N_{i})=-c_{(2d)}(i^{*}(\xi\oplus\xi))=-i^{*}c_{(2d)}(\xi\oplus\xi).

Moreover,
c_{(2d)}(\xi\oplus\xi)=(c_{1}(\xi))^{2d}+(c_{1}(\xi))^{2d}=2\alpha^{2d}
. We have then

(6.1)

\text{deg}_{k}(c_{(2d)}(T_{Y}))=\text{deg}_{k}(-2i^{*}\alpha^{2d}).

We now claim that
i_{*}i^{*}x=\alpha^{2}x
for all
x\in H\mathbb{Z}^{4d,2d}(X)
. To see this, let
1^{Y}\in H\mathbb{Z}^{0,0}(Y)
,
1^{X}\in H\mathbb{Z}^{0,0}(X)
be the respective units. Then the projection formula (Proposition
2.31
(2)) gives
i_{*}i^{*}x=i_{*}(1^{Y}\cdot i^{*}x)=i_{*}(1^{Y})\cdot x
. Next, we recall that
Y
is the zero-locus of a global section
s
of
\xi\oplus\xi
that is transverse to the zero section
s_{0}
. This gives us the transverse cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{0}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\xi\oplus\xi.}

Thus, by the push-pull formula (Proposition
2.31
(1)) and Lemma
2.35
, we have
i_{*}(1^{Y})=i_{*}i^{*}(1_{X})=s^{*}s_{0*}(1_{X})=c_{2}(\xi\oplus\xi).
Finally, the Whitney sum formula for Chern classes gives
c_{2}(\xi\oplus\xi)=c_{1}(\xi)^{2}=\alpha^{2}
,
so
i_{*}i^{*}x=\alpha^{2}\cdot x
, proving the claim.

Thus we have

s_{(2d)}(Y)\overset{\eqref{eq:degree_through_alpha}}{=}p_{Y*}(-2i^{*}\alpha^{2d})=p_{X*}i_{*}(-2i^{*}\alpha^{2d})\overset{\text{claim}}{=}p_{X*}(-\alpha^{2}\cdot 2\alpha^{2d})=(-2)\cdot{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(\alpha^{2d+2}).

∎

We can now refine Theorem
6.12
by using Lemma
6.13
and the ideas of
[
45
, Section 4]
in topology.

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
k
be an infinite field, and let
\ell
be an odd prime different from
{\operatorname{char}}k
. Then there exists a family
\{(Y_{2d},(v_{2d},\omega_{2d}))\}_{d\geq 1}
, with
Y_{2d}\in{\operatorname{Sm}}/k
proper of dimension
2d
over
k
, and
(v_{2d},\omega_{2d})\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(Y_{2d})
a virtual symplectic bundle defining a symplectic twist of
T_{Y_{2d}}
, thereby giving a class
[Y_{d},(v_{2d},\omega_{2d})]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}_{4d,2d}(k)
, such that

\nu_{\ell}(s_{2d}(Y_{2d}))=\begin{cases}0,\;\;\;\text{if}\;\;2d\neq\ell^{i}-1\;\forall\;i\\
1,\;\;\;\text{if}\;\;2d=\ell^{r}-1,\;r\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
d
be any positive integer such that
2d\neq\ell^{i}-1
for all
i
. Let

2d+2=a_{0}+a_{1}\ell+\ldots+a_{r}\ell^{r}

be the
\ell
-adic expansion of
2d+2
. Let us define

X_{2d+2}\coloneqq(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{\times a_{0}}\times(\mathbb{P}^{\ell})^{\times a_{1}}\times\ldots\times(\mathbb{P}^{\ell^{r}})^{\times a_{r}},

then
{\operatorname{dim}}(X_{2d+2})=2d+2
. Let
Y_{2d}\subset X_{2d+2}
be the zero locus of a general section of the vector bundle
\xi\oplus\xi\to X_{2d+2}
as in Construction
6.9
. Then
s_{(2d)}(Y_{2d})={\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(-2\alpha^{2d+2})
by Lemma
6.13
.
Let
\alpha_{i,j}\coloneqq c_{1}(p_{i,j}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1))
, where
p_{i,j}
is the projection of
X_{2d+2}
onto the
j
-th component of
(\mathbb{P}^{\ell^{i}})^{\times a_{i}}
, so that

\alpha=(\alpha_{0,1}+\alpha_{0,2}+\ldots+\alpha_{0,a_{0}}+\alpha_{1,1}+\ldots+\alpha_{1,a_{1}}+\ldots+\alpha_{r,a_{r}}).

In particular:

\alpha^{2d+2}=(\alpha_{0,1}+\alpha_{0,2}+\ldots+\alpha_{0,a_{0}}+\alpha_{1,1}+\ldots+\alpha_{1,a_{1}}+\ldots+\alpha_{r,a_{r}})^{a_{0}+a_{1}\ell+\ldots+a_{r}\ell^{r}}.

Since
X_{2d+2}
is a product of projective spaces, we have Künneth formula

H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2*,*}(X)\cong H^{2*,*}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{\otimes a_{0}}\otimes\ldots\otimes H^{2*,*}({\mathbb{P}}^{\ell^{r}})^{\otimes a_{r}},

with all tensor products over
{\mathbb{Z}}
, and the isomorphism given by taking the respective pullbacks and cup products. This follows by repeated application of the projective bundle formula (Theorem
2.36
). For all projective spaces
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
, the top degree term in
H^{2*,*}({\mathbb{P}}^{n})
is
H^{2n,n}({\mathbb{P}}^{n})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
, with generator
c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{n}}(1))^{n}
, and
p_{{\mathbb{P}}^{n}*}(c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{n}}(1))^{n})=1\in H{\mathbb{Z}}^{0,0}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
. Thus, through a series of uses of the push-pull formula and the projection formula (Proposition
2.31
(1)-(2)), we see that the top degree term in
H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2*,*}(X_{2d+2})
is
H{\mathbb{Z}}^{4d+4,2d+2}(X_{2d+2})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
, with generator

\alpha_{*}^{*}:=\alpha_{0,1}\cdot\alpha_{0,2}\cdots\alpha_{0,a_{0}}\cdot\alpha_{1,1}^{\ell}\cdots\alpha_{1,a_{1}}^{\ell}\cdots\alpha_{r,1}^{\ell^{r}}\cdots\alpha_{r,a_{r}}^{\ell^{r}},

and
{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(\alpha_{*}^{*})=1
. Therefore,
{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})
is the coefficient of

\alpha_{0,1}\cdot\alpha_{0,2}\cdots\alpha_{0,a_{0}}\cdot\alpha_{1,1}^{\ell}\cdots\alpha_{1,a_{1}}^{\ell}\cdots\alpha_{r,1}^{\ell^{r}}\cdots\alpha_{r,a_{r}}^{\ell^{r}}

in the expansion of
2\alpha^{2d+2}
.

The multinomial theorem gives the combinatorial formula

(x_{1}+x_{2}+\ldots x_{m})^{n}=\sum_{i_{1}+\ldots+i_{m}=n}\frac{n!}{i_{1}!i_{2}!\ldots i_{m}!}x_{1}^{i_{1}}x_{2}^{i_{2}}\ldots x_{m}^{i_{m}},

and multinomial coefficients satisfy the identity

\frac{n!}{i_{1}!i_{2}!\ldots i_{m}!}=\binom{i_{1}}{i_{1}}\binom{i_{1}+i_{2}}{i_{2}}\cdots\binom{i_{1}+i_{2}+\ldots+i_{m}}{i_{m}}.

Thus,
{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})
is the coefficient

2\cdot\frac{(2d+2)!}{1!1!\cdots 1!\ell!\cdots\ell!\ell^{2}!\cdots\ell^{r}!}=\\
=2\binom{1}{1}\binom{2}{1}\cdots\binom{a_{0}}{1}\binom{a_{0}+\ell}{\ell}\binom{a_{0}+2\ell}{\ell}\cdots\binom{a_{0}+a_{1}\ell}{\ell}\binom{a_{0}+a_{1}+\ell^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\cdots\binom{2d+2}{\ell^{r}}.

The product of the first
a_{0}
binomial coefficients is
a_{0}!
. The product of the following
a_{1}
binomial coefficients, reduced modulo
\ell
, is
a_{1}!
. And so on. Iterating this, we get

{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})\equiv\ 2a_{0}!a_{1}!\cdots a_{r}!\;\;\;\text{mod}\;\ell.

In particular,
\ell
does not divide
{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})
, so
\nu_{\ell}(s_{2d}(Y_{d}))=0
.

Let now
d
be a positive integer such that
2d=\ell^{r}-1
for some
r
. In this case, let us define

X_{2d+2}\coloneqq\mathbb{P}^{1}\times(\mathbb{P}^{\ell^{r-1}})^{\ell},

so that
{\operatorname{dim}}(X_{2d+2})=\ell^{r}+1=2d+2
, and take
Y_{2d}\subset X_{2d+2}
obtained again as in Construction
6.9
. Now we let
p_{0}
be the projection of
X_{d+2}
on the first factor
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
p_{1,j}:X_{2d+2}\to\mathbb{P}^{\ell^{r-1}}
be the projection on the
j
-th component of
(\mathbb{P}^{\ell^{r-1}})^{\ell}
. As before, we write

\alpha=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1,1}+\ldots+\alpha_{1,\ell},

and then

\alpha^{2d+2}=(\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1,1}+\ldots+\alpha_{1,\ell})^{\ell^{r}+1}.

So,
{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})
is now the coefficient of

\alpha_{0}\cdot\alpha_{1,1}^{\ell^{r-1}}\cdot\alpha_{1,2}^{\ell^{r-1}}\cdots\alpha_{1,\ell}^{\ell^{r-1}}

in the expansion of
2\alpha^{\ell^{r}+1}
. By using the multinomial theorem as before, we get

{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2})=2\binom{1}{1}\binom{1+\ell^{r-1}}{\ell^{r-1}}\binom{1+2\ell^{r-1}}{\ell^{r-1}}\cdots\binom{1+\ell^{r}}{\ell^{r-1}}.

Noting that:

\binom{1+j\ell^{r-1}}{\ell^{r-1}}=\binom{j\ell^{r-1}}{\ell^{r-1}}\frac{1+j\ell^{r-1}}{1+j\ell^{r-1}-\ell^{r-1}},

it is easy to see that
\nu_{\ell}({\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(2\alpha^{2d+2}))=1
, concluding the proof.
∎

From now on, we can adopt the following notation.

Definition 6.15
.

For
\ell
a fixed odd prime different from
\text{char}(k)
, we denote by

[Y_{2d},(v,\omega)]_{{\operatorname{MSp}}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}_{4d,2d}(k)

the class associated as in Definition
6.11
to the variety
Y_{2d}
defined in Theorem
6.14
.

Remark 6.16
.

s_{2d}(Y_{2d})
also computes
\text{deg}_{k}(c_{(2d)}(i^{*}W-(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee})))
. Indeed, Lemma
2.39
(2)-(3) gives

c_{(2d)}(\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime\vee})=i^{*}c_{(2d)}(\xi)+i^{*}c_{(2d)}(\xi^{\vee})=i^{*}\alpha^{2d}+i^{*}(-\alpha)^{2d}=\\
i^{*}(2\alpha^{2d})=i^{*}c_{(2d)}(\xi\oplus\xi)=c_{(2d)}(N_{i})=-c_{(2d)}(T_{Y_{2d}}),

and Lemma
2.39
(1) gives
c_{(2d)}(i^{*}W)=i^{*}c_{(2d)}(W)=0
because
W
is the direct sum of pullbacks
p_{j}^{*}W_{j}
on
Y_{2d}
, where
W_{j}
is a vector bundle on a space
{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{j}+1}
of dimension less than
2d
.

6.2.
Detecting generators

We start by recalling a few facts on
{\operatorname{MGL}}
. In
[
22
, Lemma 5.9]
, they give a description of the trigraded
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
, as we did in Proposition
5.8
after replacing
{\operatorname{MGL}}
with
{\operatorname{MSp}}
. In particular,
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(2u-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
is polynomial in the generators:

(6.2)

-

1\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(0,0)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
;

-

z^{\prime}_{(k)}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{0,(-2k,-k)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
, for
k\geq 1
, and
k\neq\ell^{i}-1\;\forall\;i\geq 0
;

-

h_{r}^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{1,(1-2\ell^{r},1-\ell^{r})}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})
, for
r\geq 0
.

It is known that
{\operatorname{MGL}}
is
(-1)
-connected (
[
22
, Corollary 2.3]
) and
\eta
-completed, namely
{\operatorname{MGL}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}={\operatorname{MGL}}
(
[
41
, Lemma 2.1]
). Thus, by Theorem
5.17
, one has
{\operatorname{MGL}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge}\simeq{\operatorname{MGL}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge}
. Also,
[
22
, Lemma 6.3]
shows that
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{\wedge}_{\ell})^{*}\cong({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
. Putting this together gives

({\operatorname{MGL}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\cong({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}.

Moreover,
{\operatorname{MGL}}
is a cellular spectrum (Example
4.3
), and
W_{s}({\operatorname{MGL}})
is a motivically finite type wedge of copies of
H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
(
[
22
, Proposition 6.10]
), which means that
{\operatorname{MGL}}
satisfies the conditions of Proposition
5.12
. This leads to the following.

Theorem 6.17

(
[
22
]
, Theorem 6.11, Proposition 6.12)
.

The motivic mod
\ell
Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MGL}}
is of the form

E_{2}^{s,t,u}={\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}^{s,(t-s,u)}(H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}}),H^{*,*})\Rightarrow({\operatorname{MGL}}_{H{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell}^{\wedge})^{t,u},

and the sequence converges completely to
({\operatorname{MGL}}_{\ell}^{\wedge})^{2u,u}
.

Remark 6.18
.

As noted in
[
22
, Section 6.6]
along the proof of Theorem B, the Adams spectral sequence for
{\operatorname{MGL}}
has associated filtration
F^{*}({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
, graded by
\oplus_{u}E_{\infty}^{2u,u}({\operatorname{MGL}})\simeq\oplus_{u}E_{2}^{2u,u}({\operatorname{MGL}})\coloneqq\oplus_{u}\oplus_{s}E_{2}^{s,(2u,u)}({\operatorname{MGL}})
, which is a graded
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra, with
E_{2}^{0,(0,0)}({\operatorname{MGL}})={\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
and
F^{m}(({\operatorname{MGL}}^{0})_{\ell}^{\wedge})=(\ell^{m}){\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
. Thus, the polynomial generators
z^{\prime}_{(k)}
and
h^{\prime}_{r}
in (
6.2
) lift, respectively, to polynomial generators

\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{(k)}\in F^{0}(({\operatorname{MGL}}^{-2k,-k})^{\wedge}_{\ell})\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{r}\in F^{1}(({\operatorname{MGL}}^{-2(\ell^{r}-1),-(\ell^{r}-1)})_{\ell}^{\wedge})

for the graded
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
-algebra
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
, and these generators are unique up to decomposable elements and elements in
\ell\cdot({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
.

The natural map
\Phi:{\operatorname{MSp}}\to{\operatorname{MGL}}
induces the map

\Phi_{*}:({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\to({\operatorname{MGL}}_{\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\simeq({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}.

Proposition 6.19
.

The map
