\Phi_{*}:({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\to({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots]
is injective, with image the polynomial subring with generator
x_{2},x_{4},\ldots
.

Proof.

The map
\Phi:{\operatorname{MSp}}\to{\operatorname{MGL}}
induces a map between the two respective Adams spectral sequences. By looking at the decompositions of
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MSp}})
and
H^{*,*}({\operatorname{MGL}})
given by Lemma
5.6
and
[
22
, Lemma 5.9]
, we see that the induced map between the two respective
E_{2}
-pages is an isomorphism on the summands of the
{\operatorname{Ext}}
-algebras corresponding to the duals of the summands
M_{B}u_{\omega}
, with
w\in P
, namely with
\omega
being an even (necessarily non-
\ell
adic) partition. In particular, the
E_{2}
page for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
is a split summand of the
E_{2}
page for
{\operatorname{MGL}}
. Since both spectral sequences degenerate at
E_{2}
, this implies that the map

\Phi_{*}:({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\to({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}
is injective.

We consider the filtration
F^{*}({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
associated to the spectral sequence
E({\operatorname{MSp}})
for
{\operatorname{MSp}}
. Analogously to the case of
F^{*}({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}
in Remark
6.18
, the generators
z_{(2k)}
and
h_{r}
of
E_{2}({\operatorname{MSp}})
lift to generators
\tilde{z}_{(2k)}
and
\tilde{h}_{r}
of the graded
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
algebra
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
, with
r\geq 0
,
k\geq 1
,
2k\neq\ell^{i}-i
for any
i\geq 0
, uniquely up to sums with decomposable elements and elements in
\ell\cdot({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
. We see that, through the inclusion
\Phi_{*}:({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}\hookrightarrow({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}
,
\tilde{z}_{(2k)}
and
\tilde{h}_{r}
map to the polynomial generators
\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{(2k)}
and
\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{r}
respectively, up to decomposable elements and elements in
\ell\cdot({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
. Indeed,
z_{(2k)}
is the dual of
u_{(2k)}
, hence it belongs to the summand of
E_{2}({\operatorname{MSp}})
corresponding
M_{B}u_{(2k)}
, while the elements
h_{r}
are generators of the
{\mathbb{Z}}/\ell
algebra
{\operatorname{Ext}}_{B}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell,H^{*,*})\simeq{\operatorname{Ext}}_{A^{*,*}}(M_{B},H^{*,*})
, so they belong to the summand corresponding to
M_{B}u_{(0)}
.

Thus, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra generators
\tilde{z}_{(2k)}
and
\tilde{h}_{r}
of
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
map to (possibly new) polynomial generators of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}={\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}[\{\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{(k)},\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{r}\}_{k,r}]
in all even degrees. Thus
\Phi_{*}
identifies
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
with this polynomial subalgebra of
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})_{\ell}^{\wedge}
.
∎

From the generating criterion for
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
of Proposition
2.51
, we can obtain the following one.

Proposition 6.20
.

A family of element
\{y_{2d}^{\prime}\}_{d\geq 1}
, with
y_{2d}^{\prime}\in({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell})^{-2d}
form a family of polynomial generators of
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
if and only if

c_{(2d)}(\Phi_{*}y_{2d}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\times}&\text{for}\;\;2d\neq\ell^{r}-1\;\forall r\geq 1\\
\lambda^{\prime}\cdot\ell,\;\lambda^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\times}&\text{for}\;\;2d=\ell^{r}-1,\;r\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proof.

We retain the notation used in the proof of Proposition
6.19
.

From Remark
6.18
, we see that the elements
\tilde{h}_{r}^{\prime}
, for
r\geq 1
, give generators of
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
of degrees
1-\ell^{r}
respectively, and the elements
\tilde{z}_{(k)}^{\prime}
give generators in the remaining non-zero degrees. Thus, by Proposition
2.51
, we have
\nu_{\ell}(c_{(k)}(\tilde{z}_{k}^{\prime}))=0
and
\nu_{\ell}(c_{(\ell^{r}-1)}(\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{r}))=1.
Therefore, we also have

(6.3)

\nu_{\ell}(c_{(2k)}(\Phi_{*}\tilde{z}_{2k}))=0\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\nu_{\ell}(c_{(\ell^{r}-1)}(\Phi_{*}\tilde{h}_{r}))=1.

Let
y\in({\operatorname{MGL}}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
be a polynomial generator. Then, modulo
\ell\cdot({\operatorname{MGL}}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
and decomposable elements,
y
is the product of a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-unit and a generator
\tilde{h}_{r}^{\prime}
(if
n=1-\ell^{r}
) or
\tilde{z}_{k}^{\prime}
(if
n
is not of the form
1-\ell^{r}
). Since Newton classes vanish on decomposables, we see that for
y\in({\operatorname{MGL}}^{n})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
arbitrary,
y
is a polynomial generator if and only if
\nu_{\ell}(c_{(n)}(y))=1
if
n
is of the form
1-\ell^{r}
, and
\nu_{\ell}(c_{(n)}(y))=0
if
n
is not of this form.

From Proposition
6.19
, we see that the sub-
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra
({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell})^{*}
is the polynomial sub-
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-algebra of
({\operatorname{MGL}}^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}

with polynomial generators the
\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{r}
,
r\geq 1
and the
\tilde{z}_{k}^{\prime}
for
k
even (and non-
\ell
-adic). Applying the above criterion, we see that an element
y_{2d}^{\prime}\in({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell})^{-2d}
,
d\geq 1
, is a polynomial generator of
({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell})^{*}
if and only if

\nu_{\ell}(c_{(2d)}(\Phi_{*}y_{2d}^{\prime}))=\begin{cases}1\;\;\;\;\text{for}\;\;2d=\ell^{r}-1,\;r\geq 1\\
0\;\;\;\text{for}\;\;2d\neq\ell^{r}-1\;\forall r\geq 1.\end{cases}

This gives the result.
∎

Let
X=\prod_{i=1}^{2r}\mathbb{P}^{2n_{i}+1}
and
Y\subset X
as in Construction
6.9
, with
2d:={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}Y={\operatorname{dim}}_{k}X-2
. Definition
6.11
gives the stable symplectic twist
((v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r)
of
-T_{Y}
. In order to simplify the notation, we introduce the following shorthand.

Definition 6.21
.

We let
-T_{Y}^{\prime}\in K_{0}(Y)
be the virtual vector bundle
\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{2r}+v_{Y}
.

In particular, the isomorphism
\vartheta_{Y}
of Construction
6.9
can be read as

\vartheta_{Y}:\Sigma^{-T_{Y}}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sigma^{-T_{Y}^{\prime}}1_{Y}.

Let us recall that, by Remark
2.40
,
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y})
and
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y}^{\prime})
are defined.

Proposition 6.22
.

We have

c_{(2d)}(\Phi_{*}[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}})=(-1)^{n_{Y}}{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y})),

where
n_{Y}=1+\Sigma_{i=1}^{2r}(n_{i}+1)

Proof.

We first note that, for
x\in{\operatorname{MGL}}^{-n}={\operatorname{MGL}}_{2n,n}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)
, and
c_{(n)}\in H{\mathbb{Z}}^{2n,n}({\operatorname{MGL}})
, by Remark
2.5
we have

c_{(n)}(x):=\langle c_{(n)},h(x)\rangle=c_{(n)}\circ x\in H{\mathbb{Z}}^{0,0}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)={\mathbb{Z}}.

Next, expanding the full definition of
[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
as
[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
, we have

\Phi_{*}[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}=[Y,v_{Y}+{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{2r},\vartheta_{Y}]_{\operatorname{MGL}}\in{\operatorname{MGL}}^{-2d,-d}({\operatorname{Spec}}k),

where
v_{Y}\in K_{0}(Y)
is the image of
(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})\in K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(Y)
under the evident map

K_{0}^{\operatorname{Sp}}(Y)\to K_{0}(Y)
forgetting the symplectic structure. Thus

c_{(2d)}\circ\Phi_{*}[Y,(v_{Y},\omega_{Y}),\vartheta_{Y},2r]_{\operatorname{MSp}}=c_{(2d)}\circ[Y,-T_{Y}^{\prime},\vartheta_{Y}]_{\operatorname{MGL}}.

Furthermore,
-T_{Y}^{\prime}
has virtual rank
-2d
and
\vartheta_{Y}
is a composition of
2r+1
isomorphisms of the form
{\operatorname{Anan}}_{e,-L_{1},\ldots,-L_{s}}
,
2r
for the factors
{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{1}+1}
and one coming from the rank
2
bundle
\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime}
. In particular, for each factor
{\mathbb{P}}^{2n_{i}+1}
of
X
we have
s=n_{i}+1
, and for
\xi^{\prime}\oplus\xi^{\prime}
we have
s=1
. Thus, the sum of all the indices
s
relative to the isomorphisms
{\operatorname{Anan}}_{e,-L_{1},\ldots,-L_{s}}
is
n_{Y}
, and it follows from Corollary
3.6
that

c_{(2d)}\circ[Y,-T_{Y}^{\prime},\vartheta_{Y}]_{\operatorname{MGL}}=(-1)^{n_{Y}}\cdot{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y}^{\prime}))=(-1)^{n_{Y}}\cdot{\operatorname{deg}}_{k}(c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y}^{\prime})).

Finally, up to isomorphisms,
-T_{Y}
and
-T_{Y}^{\prime}
are both (virtual) sums in
K_{0}(Y)
of classes of line bundles, where the only difference is that some of the line bundles
L_{i}
appearing in
-T_{Y}
get replaced with
L_{i}^{\vee}
. But if
-T_{Y}=\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}[L_{i}]
, with
\epsilon_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
, then by additivity of the Newton classes we have
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y})=\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}\cdot c_{1}^{H{\mathbb{Z}}}(L_{i})^{2d}
and
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y}^{\prime})=\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}\cdot c_{1}^{H{\mathbb{Z}}}(L_{i}^{\otimes\tau_{i}})^{2d}
for suitable
\tau_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
.
Since
H{\mathbb{Z}}
has additive formal group law (Example
2.37
), we have
c_{1}(L_{i}^{\otimes-1})^{2d}=(-c_{1}(L_{i}))^{2d}=c_{1}(L_{i})^{2d}
,
so
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y}^{\prime})=c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y})
, completing the proof.
∎

Let us now consider the varieties
Y_{2d}
constructed in Theorem
6.14
, and their associated symplectic classes
[Y_{2d}]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\coloneqq[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
(Definition
6.15
).

Still by Remark
2.40
and additivity of Newton classes we have
c_{(2d)}(-T_{Y_{2d}})=-c_{(2d)}(T_{Y_{2d}})
. Thus, Proposition
6.22
implies the identity

(6.4)

\nu_{\ell}(c_{(2d)}(\Phi_{*}[Y_{2d}]_{\operatorname{MSp}}))=\nu_{\ell}(s_{2d}(Y_{2d})).

The identity (
6.4
) and Theorem
6.14
tell us that the symplectic classes
[Y_{2d}]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}
satisfy the generating criterion for
({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta,\ell})^{*}
established in Proposition
6.20
.

We have then achieved the following.

Theorem 6.23
.

Let
\ell
be a fixed odd prime different from
{\operatorname{char}}(k)
, and for
d\geq 1
let
[Y_{2d}]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\coloneqq[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\in{\operatorname{MSp}}^{-4d,-2d}({\operatorname{Spec}}k)
be the symplectic classes of Definition
6.15
. Then the images of the classes
[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
in
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
give a family of polynomial generators for
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
.

Proposition 6.24
.

For each odd prime
\ell
different from
{\operatorname{char}}k
, we have

(({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta})^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\cong({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}.

Proof.

We first note that there is an injective map
(({\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta})^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\hookrightarrow({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})
. The argument is the same as in the proof of
[
22
, Lemma 6.13 (3)]
, after replacing
{\operatorname{MSL}}
with
{\operatorname{MSp}}
. To show the surjectivity, it is sufficient to show that
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
, seen as a subalgebra of
({\operatorname{MGL}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*}
through
\Phi_{*}
, is generated, as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
-module, by the images
\Phi_{*}[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
. This follows directly from Theorem
6.23
.
∎

From Proposition
6.20
, by working on the
\ell
-torsion of
({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}
for each
\ell
separately, we can deduce the following global criterion.

Theorem 6.25
.

Let
({\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{*}[1/2p]
denote the quotient of the graded ring
({{\operatorname{MSp}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{*}[1/2p]
by its maximal subgroup that is
\ell
-divisible for all odd primes
\ell\neq p
. Then
({\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{*}[1/2p]
is a polynomial ring over
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/2p]
, and a family
\{y_{2d}^{\prime}\}_{d\geq 1}
, with
y^{\prime}_{2d}\in({\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{-2d}[1/2p]
, forms a family of polynomial generators for
({\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{*}[1/2p]
if and only if

(6.5)

c_{(2d)}(\Phi_{*}y_{2d}^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}[1/2p]^{\times}&\text{for}\;\;2d\neq\ell^{r}-1,\;\;\text{for all prime}\;\ell\neq 2,p;\;\forall r\geq 1\\
\lambda^{\prime}\cdot\ell,\;\lambda^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{\times}&\text{for}\;\;2d=\ell^{r}-1,\;\;\ell\;\text{prime},\;\ell\neq 2,p;\;r\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proof.

(\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}[1/2p]
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/2p]
-module. Then for all odd prime
\ell
different from
p
, we have

(\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}[1/2p]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[1/2p]}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\cong(\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}\cong(({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\cong({\operatorname{MSp}}_{\eta,\ell}^{\wedge})^{*},

where the last isomorphism is Proposition
6.24
.

By using Proposition
6.20
as a generating criterion for
(\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}_{\eta}^{\wedge})^{*}[1/2p]\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}[1/2p]}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}
, for each
\ell
, we obtain that an element
y^{\prime}_{2d}\in{(\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{-2d}[1/2p]
is a generator if and only if it satisfies the conditions (
6.5
).
∎

Remark 6.26
.

Let us note that Theorem
6.14
also provides a family
\{[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}\}_{d\geq 1}
of polynomial generators for
({\overline{{\operatorname{MSp}}}^{\wedge}_{\eta}})^{*}[1/2p]
. If
2d=\ell^{r}-1
for some odd prime
\ell\neq p
and some
r\geq 1
, one takes the variety
Y_{2d}\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\times({\mathbb{P}}^{\ell^{r-1}})^{\times\ell}
constructed in the proof of Theorem
6.14
for that specific
\ell
, and its respective symplectic class
[Y_{2d},(v_{Y},\omega_{Y})]_{\operatorname{MSp}}
. If
2d\neq\ell^{r}-1
for all
\ell,r
, one can take the variety
Y_{2d}\subset({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{\times(2d+2)}
constructed in the same proof for any
\ell>2d+2
.
